Algorithm 8

Approximate Belief Learning for MARL with Privileged Information

Appendix C
Missing Details in
Section

3

Proof of
Proposition

3.1
:

We recall that
\bm{b}_{h}(\cdot)
is the belief of the agent about the underlying state, see
Appendix

A
for a detailed introduction. Note that
Equation

3.1
can be written as

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{\star}\in\arg\min_{\pi\in\Pi}~~\sumop\displaylimits_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})}\left[{D_{f}(\pi^{\star}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h}){\,|\,}\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h}))}\right].

Therefore, for any
h\in[H]
and
\tau_{h}
such that
\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{\prime},\mathcal{P}}(\tau_{h})>0
, we can optimize
\pi
separately for each
h\in[H]
and
\tau_{h}
as:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h}^{\star}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})}~~\mathbb{E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})}\left[{D_{f}(\pi^{\star}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h}){\,|\,}q)}\right].

Now we are ready to construct the counter-example of
\gamma
-observable POMDP
\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
H=1
,
{\mathcal{S}}=\left\{s^{1},s^{2}\right\}
,
\mathcal{A}=\left\{a^{1},a^{2}\right\}
, and
\mathcal{O}=\left\{o^{1},o^{2}\right\}
. We let
\mu_{1}=(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma},\frac{1}{2-\gamma})
,
\mathbb{O}_{1}(o^{1}{\,|\,}s^{1})=1
, and
\mathbb{O}_{1}(o^{1}{\,|\,}s^{2})=1-\gamma
,
\mathbb{O}_{1}(o^{2}{\,|\,}s^{2})=\gamma
. Therefore, it is direct to see that
\mathbb{O}_{1}
is exactly
\gamma
-observable. Most importantly, we choose
r_{1}(s^{1},a^{1})=1
,
r_{1}(s^{1},a^{2})=0
, and
r_{1}(s^{2},a^{1})=0
,
r_{1}(s^{2},a^{2})=\epsilon
.

Therefore, given such a reward function, the fully observable expert policy is given by
\pi^{\star}_{1}(a^{1}{\,|\,}s^{1})=1
and
\pi^{\star}_{1}(a^{2}{\,|\,}s^{2})=1
, i.e., choosing
a^{1}
at state
s^{1}
and
a^{2}
at state
s^{2}
deterministically. Meanwhile, by our construction, one can compute that the belief given observation
o^{1}
ensures
\bm{b}_{1}(o^{1})=\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{S}})
. Hence, the corresponding “distilled” partially observable policy under observation
o^{1}
is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{\star}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}o^{1})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\bm{b}_{1}(o^{1})}\left[{D_{f}(\pi^{\star}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{1}){\,|\,}q)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})}\frac{D_{f}(\pi^{\star}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s^{1}){\,|\,}q)+D_{f}(\pi^{\star}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s^{2}){\,|\,}q)}{2}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})}\frac{{f(1/q(a^{1}))q(a^{1})+f(0)q(a^{2})}+f(0)q(a^{1})+f(1/q(a^{2}))q(a^{2})}{2}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})}\frac{f(0)+f(1/q(a^{1}))q(a^{1})+f(1/q(a^{2}))q(a^{2})}{2},

where the last step is due to
q\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})
. Now consider the function
g(x)=xf(1/x)
for
x>0
. It is direct to compute that
g^{\prime}(x)=f(1/x)-\frac{f^{\prime}(1/x)}{x}
, and
g^{\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(1/x)}{x^{3}}\geq 0
due to the convexity of the function
f
. Thus, we conclude that
g
is also convex. By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{f(1/q(a^{1}))q(a^{1})+f(1/q(a^{2}))q(a^{2})}{2}\geq f(2/(q(a^{1})+q(a^{2})))(q(a^{1})+q(a^{2}))/2=f(2)/2,

where the equality holds uniquely when
q(a^{1})=q(a^{2})=\frac{1}{2}
for the common choice of
D_{f}
in
f
-divergence, where
f
is strictly convex
[
81
]
. This indicates that
\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{\star}(\cdot{\,|\,}o^{1})=\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal{A})
. On the other hand, combining the fact that
\bm{b}_{1}(o^{1})=\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{S}})
with
\epsilon<1
, it is direct to see that the optimal partially observable policy
\widetilde{\pi}\in\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi)
satisfies
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}(a^{1}{\,|\,}o^{1})=1
. Now we are ready to evaluate the optimality gap between
\widetilde{\pi}
and
\widehat{\pi}^{\star}
as follows

\displaystyle v^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(\widetilde{\pi})-v^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(\widehat{\pi}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})(V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1}))+\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{2})(V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{2})-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{2}))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})(V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})),

where the last step is due to the fact that
\widetilde{\pi}
is the optimal policy,
leading to the fact that
V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{2})-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{2})\geq 0
. Now it is not hard to compute that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})\geq 1-\gamma.

Meanwhile, we can evaluate that

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})=\frac{1}{2},\quad V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{4}

and correspondingly
V_{1}^{\widetilde{\pi},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})-V_{1}^{\widehat{\pi}^{\star},\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})=\frac{1-\epsilon}{4}
, implying that
v^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(\widetilde{\pi})-v^{\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(\widehat{\pi}^{\star})\geq\frac{(1-\gamma)(1-\epsilon)}{4}
.
This concludes our proof.

\hfill\square

Note that another counterexample in a similar spirit was also constructed in
[
82
]
, demonstrating that the expert policy for a poorly chosen agent-environment boundary can be useless in imitation learning, although the
\gamma
-observability property is not satisfied for the construction therein.

Remark C.1
.

The counter-example
\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}
constructed above can be also used to demonstrate the
bias
of the state-only-based value function as an estimate of the history-based value function that appeared in the policy gradient formula for POMDPs,
in the finite-horizon setting, i.e.,
\mathbb{E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})}[V^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}_{h}(s_{h})]\neq V^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}_{h}(\tau_{h})
(mirroring Theorem 4.2 of
[
47
]
). Specifically, in the counter-example above, we consider the policy
\pi
such that
\pi_{1}(a^{1}{\,|\,}o^{1})=1
and
\pi_{1}(a^{2}{\,|\,}o^{2})=1
. The state-only-based value function can be evaluated as

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(s^{1})=1,\qquad\qquad V_{1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(s^{2})=\gamma\epsilon,

which implies that
\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\bm{b}_{1}(o^{1})}[V^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}_{1}(s_{1})]=\frac{V_{1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(s^{1})+V_{1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(s^{2})}{2}=\frac{1+\gamma\epsilon}{2}
. On the other hand, it holds that
V_{1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}(o^{1})=\frac{1}{2}
, which is not equal to
\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\bm{b}_{1}(o^{1})}[V^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\epsilon}}_{1}(s_{1})]
, showing the bias of such a state-only value function.

Proof of
Example

3.3
&
Example

3.4
&
Example

3.5
&
Example

3.6
:

To see why those examples follow our
Definition

3.2
, it is indeed an immediate result of
Proposition

7.1
.

\hfill\square

Proof of
Proposition

3.7
:

Here we evaluate the computational complexity and sample complexity of each iteration
t
as follows.

Sample complexity:

The algorithm executes the policy
\pi^{t-1}
and collect
K
episodes, denoted as
\{s_{1:H+1}^{k},o_{1:H}^{k},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{a_{1:H}^{k}\}}}_{k\in[K]}
.
Thus, the sample complexity of each iteration is
\Theta(K)
.

Computational complexity for policy evaluation:

The policy evaluation of the vanilla asymmetric actor-critic is done by minimizing the Bellman error. In the finite-horizon setting with tabular parameterization, it is equivalent to performing the following update for each
h\in[H]
in a backward way and each
k\in[K]
:

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{t}(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\leftarrow(1-\alpha)Q_{h}^{t-1}(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\alpha\left({r_{h}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})|}\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{J}(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}Q_{h+1}^{t}(\tau_{h+1}^{j},s_{h+1}^{j},a_{h+1}^{j})}\right),

for some stepsize
\alpha\in(0,1)
, where
\mathcal{J}(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}):=\{j\in[K]{\,|\,}(\tau_{h}^{j},s_{h}^{j},a_{h}^{j})=(\tau_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\}
. Therefore, the computational complexity for this procedure is of
\textsc{poly}(H,K)
.

Computational complexity for policy improvement:

For tabular parameterization, computing
\nabla\log\pi_{h}^{t-1}(a_{h}^{k}{\,|\,}\tau_{h}^{k})
takes
\mathcal{O}(1)
computation. Hence the policy update in
Equation

3.2
performs
\textsc{poly}(H,K)
computation.

Meanwhile, under the exponential time hypothesis, there is no polynomial time algorithm for even planning an
\epsilon
-approximate optimal policy in
\gamma
-observable POMDPs
[
38
]
. This implies that the vanilla asymmetric actor-critic needs to take super-polynomial time to find an approximately optimal policy. This implies the corresponding sample complexity has to be super-polynomial.

Finally, we remark that even if we let the policy and the
Q
-function not depend on the entire history
\tau_{h}
but only the finite-memory
z_{h}
, the proof still holds.
The key is that whenever one only
computes
at the
sampled
history/finite-memories, i.e., updates the policy in an
asynchronous
way (in contrast to the
synchronous
one where the policies at
all
histories/finite-memories are updated), the sample and computational complexities will be coupled with the same order per iteration, which implies a super-polynomial sample complexity due to the super-polynomial computational complexity. This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

Derivation for the closed-form update
Equation

3.3
.

Note that the proximal policy optimization
[
73
]
update has the policy improvement as follows

\displaystyle\pi^{t}\leftarrow\arg\max_{\pi}\left\{L^{t-1}(\pi)-\eta^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{t-1}}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[{\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\text{KL}(\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h}){\,|\,}\pi_{h}^{t-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h}))}\right]\right\},

(C.1)

where
\eta
is some learning rate and
L^{t-1}(\pi)
is a first-order approximation of the expected accumulated rewards at
\pi^{t-1}
:

\displaystyle L^{t-1}(\pi):=v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi^{t-1})+\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{t-1}}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[{\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\left\langle Q_{h}^{t-1}(\tau_{h},s_{h},\cdot),\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})-\pi_{h}^{t-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})\right\rangle}\right].

By plugging
L^{t-1}(\pi)
into
Equation

C.1
, with simple algebric manipulations, we prove that:

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})\propto\pi_{h}^{t-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})\exp\left({\eta\mathbb{E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})}\left[{Q_{h}^{t-1}(\tau_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right]}\right).

Appendix D
Missing Details in
Section

4

Proof of
Lemma

4.4
:

The proof follows by the assumption that the total cumulative reward is at most
H
,

\displaystyle v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi)\geq{\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)\mathbbm{1}[\forall h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)=s_{h}]}\right]

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi^{E}}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)\mathbbm{1}[\forall h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)=s_{h}]}\right]+{\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi^{E}}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)\mathbbm{1}[\exists h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}]}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-{\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi^{E}}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)\mathbbm{1}[\exists h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}]}\right]

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi^{E}}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)}\right]-{\mathbb E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi^{E}}\left[{\left({\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}r_{h}}\right)\mathbbm{1}[\exists h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}]}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi^{E})-H\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{\exists h:\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi^{E})-H\epsilon,

which completes the proof.

\hfill\square

Proof of
Theorem

4.5
:

For each step
h\in[H]
we define
D_{h}
to be the distribution over the underlying state
s_{h-1}
at step
h-1
, taken action
a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{A}
based on
\pi^{E}
, the underlying state transitioned to
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
and the observation
o_{h}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})
.
We remind that we include at step zero, a dummy state-observation pair
(s_{0},o_{0})
, for notational convenience.
Formally,
D_{h}
is defined as

D_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h}):=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\left[{s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h}}\right].

We first use union bound to decompose the probability that we incorrectly decode,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{\exists h\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right].

(D.1)

For each
h\in[H]
, we can use
M
episodes to collect
M
samples from the distribution
D_{h}
.
In addition, since state
s_{H+1}
is dummy, we need not to collect episodes for
D_{H+1}
.
Denote the set of collected samples by
\widehat{D}^{M}_{h}
. We define the decoding
g_{h}
for step
h\in[H]
as follows:

g_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h})=\left\{s_{h}\mid(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\in\widehat{D}^{M}_{h}\right\}.

Observe that by
Definition

3.2
,
\{s_{h}\mid(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\in\widehat{D}^{M}_{h}\}
is either the empty set or contains only a single elements, in which case, it is true that
g_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h})=\psi_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h})
(
\psi
is the real decoding function, see
Definition

3.2
). Moreover, we slightly abuse the notation and let
\widetilde{D}_{h}^{M}
denote the empirical distribution induced by the samples in
\widehat{D}_{h}^{M}
. Thus, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
and setting
M=\Theta\left(\frac{A\cdot O\cdot S+\log(H/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
for each step
h\in[H]
, we obtain the following by the result in
[
83
]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)=\emptyset}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\notin\widehat{D}^{M}_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{u\in\mathrm{supp}(D_{h})}\mathbb{P}_{u^{\prime}\sim D_{h}}[u=u^{\prime}]\mathbbm{1}[u\notin\mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{D}_{h}^{M})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{TV}(D_{h},\widetilde{D}_{h}^{M})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon.

Thus, by union bound, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have that for each step
h\in[H]
,

\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]\leq\epsilon,

which in combination with
Equation

D.1
concludes the proof. Finally, we note that we used a total of
\Theta\left(H\cdot\frac{A\cdot O\cdot S+\log(H/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
episodes from the POMDP, and the computational time was
\textsc{poly}(H,A,O,S,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)).

\hfill\square

Appendix E
Provably Efficient Expert Policy Distillation with Function Approximation

We now turn our attention to the rich-observation setting under our deterministic filter condition.
Definition

3.2
motivates us to consider only succinct policies that incorporate an auxiliary parameter representing the most recent state, as well as the most recent observations and actions. To handle the large observation space, we further assume that for each step
h\in[H]
, the agent selects a decoding function
g_{h}
from a family of
multi-class classifiers

\mathcal{F}_{h}\subset\{{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{O}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}\}
. For the function class
\mathcal{F}_{h}
, we make the standard realizability assumption. We formally summarize our assumptions in
Assumption

E.1
.

Assumption E.1
.

We consider a POMDP that satisfies
Definition

3.2
. In addition, to derive learning algorithms that do not dependent on
O
, for each step
h\in[H]
, we assume that we have access to a class of functions
\mathcal{F}_{h}:{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{O}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}
such that the perfect decoding function
\psi_{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{h}
.

We aim for our final bounds to depend on a complexity measure of the function class
\mathcal{F}=\{\mathcal{F}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
rather than the cardinality of the observation space
\mathbb{O}
. We utilize the Daniely and Shalev-Shwartz-Dimension (DS Dimension) (
Theorem

E.2
), which characterizes the PAC learnability for multi-class classification
[
84
]
. Defining the DS dimension is beyond the scope of our paper; we direct interested readers to
[
84
]
for further details. For intuition, readers can think of the DS Dimension as a certificate of PAC learnability without loss of intuition.

Theorem E.2

(Theorem 1 in
[
84
]
)
.

Consider a family of multi-class classifiers
\mathcal{F}{}
that map features in space
x\in\mathcal{X}
to labels in space
y\in\mathcal{Y}
.
Moreover, assume there is a joint probability distribution
D
over features in
\mathcal{X}
and labels in
\mathcal{Y}
, and that there exists
g^{*}\in\mathcal{F}{}
such that for each
(x,y)\in\mathrm{supp}(D)
,
g^{*}(x)=y
.
Given
n
samples from
D
, there exists an algorithm that with probability at least
1-\delta
outputs
\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{F}{}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{(x,y)\sim D}[\widetilde{g}(x)\neq y]\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{{d^{3/2}_{DS}(\mathcal{F}{})}+\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}{n}\right),

where
d_{DS}(\mathcal{F}{})
denotes the Daniely and Shalev-Shwartz-Dimension of the function class
\mathcal{F}
.

We are now ready to present the main theorem of this section.

Theorem E.3
.

Consider a POMDP
\mathcal{P}
that satisfies
Definition

3.2
, a policy
\pi^{E}\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
, and let
\{\mathcal{F}_{h}\subseteq\{{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{O}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}\}\}_{h\in[H]}
be the decoding function class, and
\psi_{h}\in\mathcal{F}_{h}
for each
h\in[H]
, i.e.,
\{\mathcal{F}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
is realizable. Then given access to the classification oracle of
[
85
]
, there exists an algorithm learning the decoding function
\{g_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
such that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for each step
h\in[H]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{\exists h\in[H]:g_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]\leq\epsilon,

using
\mathcal{O}\left({\frac{H^{2}\left({\max_{h\in[H]}d_{DS}^{3/2}(\mathcal{F}_{h})+\log\left({\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\right)}{\epsilon}}\right)
episodes, where
d_{DS}(\mathcal{F}_{h})
is the Daniely and Shalev-Shwartz-Dimension of
\mathcal{F}_{h}

[
84
]
.

Combining
Theorem

E.3
and
Lemma

4.4
, we obtain the final polynomial sample complexity in this function approximation setting, using classification (supervised learning) oracles (c.f.
Table

1
).

Proof of
Theorem

E.3
:

For each step
h\in[H]
,
we define
D_{h}
to be the distribution over the underlying state
s_{h-1}
at step
h-1
, taken action
a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{A}
from
\pi^{E}
, the underlying state transitioned to
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
and the hallucinated observation
o_{h}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})

(we remind readers that for step
0
, we use dummy state
s_{0}
and action
a_{0}
)
.
Formally, the probability that the sequence
(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})
is sampled from
D_{h}
equals to

\displaystyle D_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h}):=\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\left[{s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h}}\right].

We first use union bound to decompose the misclassification error,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{\exists h\in[H]:\widetilde{g}_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{\pi^{E},\mathcal{P}}\ \left[{\widetilde{g}_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{\widetilde{g}_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right].

(E.1)

For each
h\in[H]
, we can use
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({\frac{H}{\epsilon}\cdot\left({\max_{h\in[H]}d^{3/2}_{DS}(\mathcal{F}_{h})+\log\left({\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\right)}\right)
episodes to collect
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({\frac{H}{\epsilon}\cdot\left({\max_{h\in[H]}d^{3/2}_{DS}(\mathcal{F}_{h})+\log\left({\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\right)}\right)
samples from distribution
D_{h}
. Hence,
by
Theorem

E.2
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{\widetilde{g}_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]\leq\frac{\epsilon}{H}.

Thus, by union bound, with probability at least
1-\delta
, using a total of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({\frac{H^{2}}{\epsilon}\cdot\left({\max_{h\in[H]}d^{3/2}_{DS}(\mathcal{F}_{h})+\log\left({\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\right)}\right)
episodes we have that,

\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}_{(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h},s_{h})\sim D_{h}}\ \left[{\widetilde{g}_{h}\left({s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h}}\right)\neq s_{h}}\right]\leq\epsilon,

which in combination with
Equation

E.1
concludes the proof.

\hfill\square

Appendix F
Missing Details in
Section

5

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
:
Let
\pi^{*}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\pi\in\Pi^{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}}\left[V_{1}^{\pi}(s_{1})\right]
,
where we define
V^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1}):=\mathbb{E}_{o_{1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{1}),a_{1}\sim\pi_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}o_{1})}[Q_{h}^{\pi}(z_{1}=(o_{1}),s_{1},a_{1})]
.
We first note the following equation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]+\frac{1}{T}\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}\left({\widetilde{V}^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})-V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}\left({V^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})-\widetilde{V}^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})}\right).

(F.1)

Next, we make use of the performance difference lemma
[
86
,
75
,
72
]
on the extended space
\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}(\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}})
.

Definition F.1
.

Consider the class of policies
\Pi^{PL}
such that at step
h\in[H]
, the policies in
\Pi^{PL}
take an action based on finite memory up to this step and the use of the underlying state, e.g., for each policy
\pi_{1:H}\in\Pi^{PL}
,
\pi_{h}:\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
.

Observation 1
.

Note that
\Pi^{L}\subseteq\Pi^{PL}
.

Lemma F.2

(Performance difference Lemma
[
86
,
75
,
72
]
; see e.g., Lemma 1 in
[
72
]
)
.

For any pair of policies
\pi=\{\pi_{h}\}_{h\in[H]},\pi^{\prime}=\{\pi^{\prime}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}\in\Pi^{PL}
,
and approximation of the
Q
-function of policy
\pi
, we have that
for each state
s_{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu_{1})
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{1}(z_{1},s_{1})-V^{\pi^{\prime}}_{1}(z_{1},s_{1})}}

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mid z_{1}}}}\left[{\left\langle{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot),\pi_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})-\pi^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mid z_{1}}}}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})-{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\left[{r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})}\right]}\right],

where
\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h})={\mathbb E}_{a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h})}[\widetilde{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})]
.

Setting
\pi=\pi^{t}\in\Pi^{L}\subseteq\Pi^{LP}
,
and
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{*}\in\Pi^{L}\subseteq\Pi^{LP}
, where we remind that
\pi^{*}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\pi\in\Pi^{L}}V_{1}^{\pi}(s_{1})
,
and for each
z_{h}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
and
h\in[H]
, we abuse the notation by letting
\pi^{t}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})=\pi^{t}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})
and
\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})=\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})
for all
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

The above formulation is thus simplified to

\displaystyle{\mathbb E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})-V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot),\pi^{t}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})-{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\left[{r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot),\pi^{t}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})}\right\rangle}\right],

where in the inequality above we used
Lemma

F.3
.
Since our policy does not depend on the realized underlying state
s_{h}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})-V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\overline{\tau}_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot),\pi^{t}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h},s_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}(\tau_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\langle{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}(\tau_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right]-{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[\left\|{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}(\tau_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right]-{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right]\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]-2\cdot H\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[d_{TV}(\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h}),\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h}))\right].

The last inequality follows by
Lemma

F.3
.
By averaging we get,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi^{t}}_{1}(s_{1})]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]+\frac{1}{T}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-2\cdot H\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[d_{TV}(\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h}),\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h}))\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]+\frac{H}{T}\max_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[{\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-2\cdot H^{2}\cdot\max_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[d_{TV}(\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h}),\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h}))\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi^{*}}_{1}(s_{1})]-\frac{2H\sqrt{H\log\left({\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}\right)}}{\sqrt{T}}-2\cdot H^{2}\cdot\max_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau_{h}\sim\pi^{*}}\left[d_{TV}(\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h}),\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h}))\right],

where the last inequality follows since for fixed
h\in[H]
and
z_{h}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}
, the agent updates her policy on memory
z_{h}
according to MWU on feedback
\left\{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a)}\right]\right\}_{a\in\mathcal{A}}
, and thus, the accumulate regret is bounded by (Section 4.3 in
[
87
]
):

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{t\in[T]}\left\langle{{\mathbb E}_{s_{h}\sim\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}(z_{h})}\left[{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi^{t}}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},\cdot)}\right],\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot\mid z_{h})-\pi_{h}^{t}(\cdot\mid z_{h})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\log\left({\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}\right)}{\eta}+\eta\cdot T\cdot\left\|Q^{\pi^{t}}_{h}\left({z_{h},s_{h},\cdot}\right)\right\|_{+\infty}\leq\frac{\log\left({\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}\right)}{\eta}+\eta\cdot T\cdot H=2\sqrt{T\cdot H\log\left({\left|{\mathcal{A}}\right|}\right)}.

The proof follows by combining
Equation

F.1
and the inequality above. Finally, to achieve the near optimality in the class of
\Pi^{L}
, we bound the optimistic estimate using
Equation

F.2
in
Lemma

F.3
, and its global
near-
optimality for a large enough
L
under
\gamma
-observability is a direct consequence of
Theorem 4.1 in

[
38
]
.
\hfill\square

Lemma F.3

(Optimistic
Q
-function - adapted from
[
35
]
)
.

Given a policy
\pi\in\Pi^{L}
, and a parameter
M\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\{\widetilde{Q}_{h}^{\pi}:\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,H]\}_{h\in[H]}
be the output of
12
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
:
~~\forall z_{h}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle H-h+1\geq\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})\geq{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\left[{r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})]\leq O\left(H^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\max(O,S)\cdot S\cdot A}{M}\cdot\log\left(\frac{S\cdot A}{\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{M\cdot S\cdot A\cdot H}{\delta}\right)}\right),

(F.2)

where
V^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})=\mathbb{E}_{o_{1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{1}),a_{1}\sim\pi_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}o_{1})}[Q_{h}^{\pi}(z_{1}=(o_{1}),s_{1},a_{1})]
,
\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})=\mathbb{E}_{o_{1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{1}),a_{1}\sim\pi_{1}(\cdot{\,|\,}o_{1})}[\widetilde{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(z_{1}=(o_{1}),s_{1},a_{1})]
and

\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h})={\mathbb E}_{a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h})}[\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})]
. Moreover,
12
needs a total of
H\cdot M
episodes from POMDP
\mathcal{P}
and runs in time
\textsc{poly}(H,M,S,A^{L},O^{L})
.

Proof.

For each step
h\in[H]
, collect
M
trajectories with states using policy
\pi
on POMDP
\mathcal{P}
and let
D_{h}=\{\overline{\tau}^{(i)}\}_{i\in[M]}
be those collected trajectories. Define the empirical transition, observation and reward distribution as follows:

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})=

\displaystyle\big|\{\overline{\tau}=(s_{1}^{\prime},o_{1}^{\prime},a_{1}^{\prime},r_{1}^{\prime}\ldots,s_{h}^{\prime},o_{h}^{\prime},a_{h}^{\prime},r_{h}^{\prime})\in D_{h}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad:(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})=(s^{\prime}_{h},a^{\prime}_{h},s^{\prime}_{h+1})\}\big|,

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}}N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1}),

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h})=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}}N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h},o_{h})=

\displaystyle\big|\{\overline{\tau}=(s_{1}^{\prime},o_{1}^{\prime},a_{1}^{\prime},r_{1}^{\prime}\ldots,s_{h}^{\prime},o_{h}^{\prime},a_{h}^{\prime},r_{h}^{\prime})\in D_{h}:(s_{h},o_{h})=(s^{\prime}_{h},o^{\prime}_{h})\}\big|,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})}{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(o_{h}\mid s_{h})=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{h}(s_{h},o_{h})}{N_{h}(s_{h})}.

Set
\delta_{1}=\frac{\delta}{2\cdot S\cdot(A+1)}
.
By
[
83
]
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for each step
h\in[H]
, state
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
and action
a\in\mathcal{A}
with probability at least
1-\delta_{1}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right),

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot\mid s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot\mid s_{h})\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h}),1)}}\right).

For the rest of the proof, we condition on this event. By union bound, this happens with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
. We define the optimistic
Q
-function recursively as follows for a memory-state pair
(z_{h},s_{h})\in\mathcal{Z}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{H+1}(z_{H+1},s_{H+1},\cdot)=0,\qquad\qquad\forall z_{H+1}\in\mathcal{Z}_{H+1},s_{H+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})=\min\Bigg(H-h+1,{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})]+r(s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right)+{\mathbb E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h+1}(s_{h+1}),1)}}\right)\Bigg),

where
\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})={\mathbb E}_{a_{h+1}\sim\pi_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h+1})}[\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1},a_{h+1})]
.
Hence the time complexity of our algorithm is
\textsc{poly}(H,M,S,A^{L},O^{L})
. To prove the first condition, we fix step
h\in[H],z_{h}\in\mathcal{Z}_{h},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
and state
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
and consider the case where
\widetilde{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})=H-h+1
. In this case, since by assumption on the POMDP
\mathcal{P}
,
r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\leq 1
, and by definition of
\widetilde{Q}_{h+1}^{\pi}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\leq H-h
, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})=1+H-h\geq\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})].

If
\widetilde{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})\neq H-h+1
, observe that:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})=

\displaystyle{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})]+r(s_{h},a_{h})+H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h+1}(s_{h+1}),1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})],

and hence,
\{\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
satisfies the first condition. Moreover, it holds that:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h},a_{h})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})]+2H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\cdot{\mathbb E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h+1}(s_{h+1}),1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})]+6H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}H\cdot\min\left(2,C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h+1}(s_{h+1}),1)}}\right).

Thus, it holds that:

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h})-V^{\pi}_{h}(z_{h},s_{h})

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h}),\\
s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})-V^{\pi}_{h+1}(z_{h+1},s_{h+1})]+6\cdot C\cdot H\cdot{\mathbb E}_{a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h})}\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\cdot C\cdot H\cdot{\mathbb E}_{{\begin{subarray}{c}{a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}z_{h})},\\
s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\end{subarray}}}\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h+1}(s_{h+1}),1)}}.

Thus, we conclude that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V_{1}^{\pi}(s_{1})]}}\leq{\mathbb E}_{\tau=(s_{1},a_{1},\ldots,s_{H+1})\sim\pi}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}8\cdot H\cdot C\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\max(S,O)\log(1/\delta_{1})}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=8\cdot H\sqrt{\max(O,S)\cdot\log(1/\delta_{1})}\cdot C\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau=(s_{1},a_{1},\ldots,s_{H+1})\sim\pi}\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right].

To finish the proof, we make use of the following lemma.

Lemma F.4

(Lemma 6 in
[
35
]
)
.

For each step
h\in[H]
, and state-action pair
(s_{h},a_{h})\in{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}
, with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
:

\sqrt{\frac{1}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}=O\left(\sqrt{\frac{S\cdot A\log(M/\delta_{2})}{M}}\right).

By setting
\delta_{2}=\frac{\delta}{2\cdot S\cdot A\cdot H}
, and taking union bound we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[\widetilde{V}^{\pi}_{1}(s_{1})]-\mathbb{E}_{s_{1}\sim\mu_{1}}[V_{1}^{\pi}(s_{1})]}}

\displaystyle\quad=8\sqrt{\max(O,S)\cdot\log(1/\delta_{1})}\cdot C\cdot\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}{\mathbb E}_{\tau=(s_{1},a_{1},\ldots,s_{H+1})\sim\pi}\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{\max(N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq O\left(H^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\max(O,S)\cdot S\cdot A}{M}\cdot\log\left(\frac{S\cdot A}{\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{M\cdot S\cdot A\cdot H}{\delta}\right)}\right).

∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.2
:

The proof of
Theorem

5.2
follows by combining
Theorem

F.5
and
Theorem

F.6
below.
Theorem

F.5
proves that we can approximately learn a POMDP model
\mathcal{P}
computationally and sample efficiently, thanks to the privileged information.

Theorem F.5
.

Fix any
\epsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
.
Algorithm
4
can learn the approximate POMDP model with transition
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{1:H}

and emission
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{1:H}
such that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for any policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
and
h\in[H]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[{\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}}\right]\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{O(\epsilon)}},

using
\textsc{poly}(S,A,H,O,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log(\frac{1}{\delta}))
episodes
in time
\textsc{poly}(S,A,H,O,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log(\frac{1}{\delta}))
.

Proof.

Note that by
Lemma

F.11
, it suffices
to consider only
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
as the optimal value for policies in
\Pi^{\text{gen}}
can be achieved by those in
\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
(by considering

r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}):=\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}
).
For each
h\in[H]
and
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
, we define

\displaystyle p_{h}(s_{h})=\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}d_{h}^{\pi}(s_{h}).

Fix
\epsilon_{1}>0
, we define
\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})=\{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}{\,|\,}p_{h}(s_{h})\geq\epsilon_{1}\}
.
By the guarantee of the EULER algorithm from
[
88
,
89
]
, one can learn a policy
\Psi(h,s_{h})
with sample complexity
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{S^{2}AH^{4}}{\epsilon_{1}})
such that
d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})\geq\frac{p_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
for each
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
with probability
1-\delta_{1}
. Now we assume this event holds for any
h\in[H]
and
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
. For each
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
, we have executed in
Algorithm

4
the policy
\Psi(h,s_{h})
followed by an action
a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
for
N
episodes, and denote the number of episodes that
s_{h}
and
a_{h}
are visited as
N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})
. Then with probability
1-e^{N\epsilon_{1}/8}
,
N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\geq\frac{Np_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
by Chernoff bound.
Now conditioned on this event, we are ready to evaluate the following: for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h},a_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}(s_{h})\pi_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1}),a_{h}}d_{h}^{\pi}(s_{h})\pi_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1}),a_{h}}d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})\pi_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}\sqrt{d^{{\Psi(h,s_{h})}}_{h}(s_{h})}\sqrt{\frac{2S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+S\sqrt{\frac{2\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}},

where the first inequality follows by
[
83
]
with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
, and the second inequality uses
d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})\geq\frac{p_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
for all
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
.
Similarly, for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}d_{h}^{\pi}(s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}\sqrt{d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})}\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{2SO\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}},

where similarly the first inequality follows by
[
83
]
with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
, and the second inequality uses
d_{h}^{\Psi(h,s_{h})}(s_{h})\geq\frac{p_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
for all
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
.
Therefore, by a union bound, all the high probability events above hold with probability

\displaystyle 1-SH\delta_{1}-SHAe^{-N\epsilon_{1}/8}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}SAH\delta_{2}.

Therefore, we can choose
N=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{S^{2}+SO}{\epsilon^{2}})
and
\epsilon_{1}=\Theta(\frac{\epsilon}{S})
,
leading to the total sample complexity

\displaystyle SHA\left(N+\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{S^{3}AH^{4}}{\epsilon}\right)\right)=\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{S^{2}AHO+S^{3}AH}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{S^{4}A^{2}H^{5}}{\epsilon}\right),

(F.3)

which completes the proof.
∎

Now with such a model learned in a reward-free way, we are ready to present our main result for approximate belief learning.

Theorem F.6
.

Consider a
\gamma
-observable POMDP
\mathcal{P}
(c.f.
Assumption

2.5
), an
\epsilon>0
, approximate transition and emission
\{\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
and
\{\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}

learned from

Algorithm

4

that ensure that
for any
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
and
h\in[H]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[{\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{H}\right).

Then we can construct in time
{\textsc{poly}(H,A,S,O,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log\frac{1}{\delta})}
a belief
\{\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}:\mathcal{Z}_{h}\rightarrow\Delta({\mathcal{S}})\}_{h\in[H]}
with no further samples.
In addition, if the parameter in
Algorithm

4
satisfies
N=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{O\log(SH/\delta)}{\gamma^{2}\epsilon_{1}})
and our class of finite memory policies
\Pi^{L}
satisfies
L\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(\gamma^{-4}\log(S/\epsilon)
, then for any
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}

and
h\in[H]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}\leq{\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)}.

Proof.

We firstly consider the following simple while important fact:
for the estimated emission
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}
, its observability can be evaluated as

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\top}(b-b^{\prime})\|_{1}\geq\|\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\top}(b-b^{\prime})\|_{1}-\|(\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\top}-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\top})(b-b^{\prime})\|_{1}\geq(\gamma-\|\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}-\mathbb{O}_{h}\|_{\infty})\|b-b^{\prime}\|_{1},

for any
b,b^{\prime}\in\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
and
\|\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}-\mathbb{O}_{h}\|_{\infty}:=\max_{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}
. Therefore, if one can ensure that the emission at
any
state
s_{h}
is learned accurately in the sense that
\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}
, we can conclude that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}
is also
\gamma/2
-observable. However, the key challenge here is that there could exist some states
s_{h}
that can only be visited
with a low probability no matter what exploration policy is used
. Therefore, emissions at such states may not be learned accurately. To address this issue, our key technique is to
redirect
the transition probability into states that cannot be explored sufficiently to some highly visited states, in a new POMDP that is close to the original one in
generating the beliefs
.

Specifically, first, for any
\epsilon_{1}>0
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{low}}_{h}:=\left\{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}{\,\bigg|\,}\frac{N_{h}(s_{h})}{N}<\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2}\right\},\quad{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{high}}_{h}:={\mathcal{S}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{low}}_{h}.

By Chernoff bound, with probability at least
1-Se^{-N\epsilon_{1}/8}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{low}}_{h}\subseteq\{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}{\,|\,}p_{h}(s_{h})<\epsilon_{1}\},

where
p_{h}(s_{h}):=\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}d_{h}^{\pi}(s_{h})
. To see the reason, we notice that for any
s_{h}
such that
p_{h}(s_{h})\geq\epsilon_{1}
, with probability
1-e^{-N\epsilon_{1}/8}
, it holds that
\frac{N_{h}(s_{h})}{N}\geq\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2}
. Therefore, the last step is by taking a union bound for all
s_{h}
. Now with
{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{low}}_{h}
defined, we are ready to construct a truncated POMDP
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
such that for each
h\in[H]
,
we define the transition as

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{T}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h+1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{low}}}\mathbb{T}_{h}(s_{h+1}^{\prime}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}{|{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{high}}|},~~~\forall s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},s_{h+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{high}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle:=0,~~~\forall s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},s_{h+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{low}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}.

Meanwhile, for the initial state distribution, we define

\displaystyle\mu_{1}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{1})

\displaystyle:=\mu_{1}(s_{1})+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{low}}}\mu_{1}(s_{1}^{\prime})}{|{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{high}}|},~~~\forall s_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{high}},

\displaystyle\mu_{1}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{1})

\displaystyle:=0,~~~\forall s_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{low}}.

For emission, we simply define

\displaystyle\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h}):=\mathbb{O}_{h}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h}),~~~\forall h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},o_{h}\in\mathcal{O}_{h}.

Finally, we define the rewards for
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
arbitrarily. Now we examine the total variation distance between the trajectory distributions in
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
.
For any policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})\leq\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{h}),

for any
\overline{\tau}_{h}\in\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{h}^{\text{high}}:=\{(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1}){\,|\,}s_{h}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h^{\prime}}^{\text{high}},\forall h^{\prime}\in[h]\}
,
since some rarely visited states’ probability has been redirected to the highly visited ones in
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
. Meanwhile, it holds by a union bound that for any
h\in[H]

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{h}\notin\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{h}^{\text{high}})\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{h^{\prime}\in[h]}\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{h^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{low}}_{h^{\prime}})\leq HS\epsilon_{1}.

Therefore, by noticing that
\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})=0
for any
\overline{\tau}_{h}\notin\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{h}^{\text{high}}
and
h\in[H]
, the total variation distance between the trajectory distributions in
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{h}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})|\leq 2HS\epsilon_{1}.

(F.4)

On the other hand, by
Equation

F.9
of
Lemma

F.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}[\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}]\leq 2\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{h}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})|\leq 4HS\epsilon_{1}.

(F.5)

With such an intermediate quantity
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
, we define the transition of its approximate version
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
as follows:
we define the transition as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h+1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{low}}}\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}^{\prime}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}{|{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{high}}|},~~~\forall s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},s_{h+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{high}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle:=0,~~~\forall s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},s_{h+1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h+1}^{\text{low}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}.

Meanwhile, for the initial state distribution, we define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{1})

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mu}_{1}(s_{1})+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{low}}}\widehat{\mu}_{1}(s_{1}^{\prime})}{|{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{high}}|},~~~\forall s_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{high}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{\text{trunc}}(s_{1})

\displaystyle:=0,~~~\forall s_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\text{low}}.

For emission, we define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h}):=\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h}),\quad\forall h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},o_{h}\in\mathcal{O}_{h}.

Now we define
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\text{sub}}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}|\times O}
to be the sub-matrix of
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}
, where we only keep those rows of
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}
that correspond to the states in
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}
. Similarly, we define
\mathbb{O}^{\text{sub}}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}|\times O}
to be the sub-matrix of
\mathbb{O}_{h}
, where we only keep those rows of
\mathbb{O}_{h}
that correspond to the states in
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}
. It is direct to see that
\mathbb{O}^{\text{sub}}
is still
\gamma
-observable. Meanwhile, we notice that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\text{sub}}-\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\text{sub}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{high}}_{h}}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\leq\max_{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{high}}_{h}}\sqrt{\frac{O\log(SH/\delta)}{N_{h}(s_{h})}}\leq\max_{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\text{high}}_{h}}\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(SH/\delta)}{N\epsilon_{1}}},

where the first inequality is by
Lemma

H.9
, and the second inequality is by the definition of
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}
. Therefore, if we take

\displaystyle N\geq\frac{8O\log(SH/\delta)}{\gamma^{2}\epsilon_{1}},

it is guaranteed that
\|\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\text{sub}}-\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\text{sub}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}
. Therefore, we conclude that
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\text{sub}}
is also
\gamma/2
-observable.

Now we are ready to examine
\widehat{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime,\text{trunc}}
. We firstly define the following POMDP
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
, which essentially deletes all states in
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{low}}
from the state space of
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
at each step
h
, which does not affect the trajectory distribution as they were not reachable in
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
. Notice that the emission of
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
is exactly
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}^{\text{sub}}
, implying that
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
is a
\gamma/2
-observable POMDP. Therefore, for policy class with finite memory
\Pi^{L}
with
L\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(\gamma^{-4}\log(S/\epsilon)
, by Theorem 4.1 in
[
22
]
,
it is guaranteed that for any
\pi\in\Pi
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}}\|\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{sub}}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\prime,\text{sub}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}\leq\epsilon,

where
\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{sub}}_{h}(\tau_{h}),\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\prime,\text{sub}}_{h}(z_{h})\in\Delta({\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}})
. Now we claim that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}}\|\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}\leq\epsilon,

(F.6)

where we define
\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\in\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
by
augmenting

\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\prime,\text{sub}}_{h}(z_{h})
with
0
for states from
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{low}}
. To see the reason, we notice that simulating
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
is exactly equivalent to simulating
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
, and that
\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})(s_{h})=0
for
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{low}}
,
\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})(s_{h})=\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{sub}}_{h}(\tau_{h})(s_{h})
for
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{high}}
.

For the total variation distance between the trajectory distributions in
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
, it holds that by
Lemma

F.9

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{T}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}\left[\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right],

where the last step is by
Lemma

F.7
.

Now by
Equation

F.4
, we have

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{h}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}\left[\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{h}8HS\epsilon_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+8H^{2}S\epsilon_{1},

where the last step is by
Theorem

F.5
. Hence, by Lemma
F.9
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+8H^{2}S\epsilon_{1},

(F.7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}\leq\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{2\epsilon}}}{3}+16H^{2}S\epsilon_{1}.

(F.8)

Finally, we are ready to prove

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\|\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}_{\pi}\|\bm{b}_{h}^{\text{trunc}}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}}_{\pi}\|\widehat{\bm{b}}^{\text{trunc}}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}(z_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4\|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}\|_{1}+2\|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}}-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle{\leq\mathcal{O}(H^{2}S\epsilon_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)}.

Therefore, by setting
\epsilon_{1}=\Theta\left(\frac{\epsilon}{H^{2}S}\right)
, we prove our lemma.
Observe that our algorithm needed no further samples. The computational complexity follows by computing belief
\bm{b}^{\text{apx}}_{h}
on POMDP
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
using finite-memory policies of size
\widetilde{\Theta}(\gamma^{-4}\log(S/\epsilon))
. For the final sample complexity, we only need to ensure
N=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{O\log(SH/\delta)}{\gamma^{2}\epsilon_{1}})
in
Equation

F.3
, thus concluding our proof.
∎

We conclude the proof of
Theorem

5.2
by combining
Theorem

F.5
and
Theorem

F.6
.

\hfill\square

F.1
Supporting Technical Lemmas

In the following, we provide some technical lemmas and their proofs.

Lemma F.7
.

Fix
n>0
and an index set
S\subseteq[n]
. For two sequences
x_{1:n}
and
y_{1:n}
such that
x_{i},y_{i}\in[0,1]
for
i\in[n]
and
\sumop\displaylimits_{i}x_{i}=1,\sumop\displaylimits_{i}y_{i}=1
, we define

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{i}

\displaystyle=x_{i}+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in S}x_{j}}{n-|S|},\quad\forall i\notin S;\qquad\qquad\widehat{x}_{i}=0,\quad\forall i\in S.

We define
\widehat{y}_{1:n}
similarly. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i}|x_{i}-y_{i}|\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{i}|\widehat{x}_{i}-\widehat{y}_{i}|.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i}|\widehat{x}_{i}-\widehat{y}_{i}|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\notin S}|\widehat{x}_{i}-\widehat{y}_{i}|=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\notin S}\left|{x}_{i}+\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in S}x_{j}}{n-|S|}-{y}_{i}-\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in S}y_{j}}{n-|S|}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(n-|S|)\left|\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in S}x_{j}}{n-|S|}-\frac{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in S}y_{j}}{n-|S|}\right|+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\notin S}|x_{i}-y_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{i}|x_{i}-y_{i}|,

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.8
.

Fix two finite sets
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
and two joint distributions
P_{1},P_{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})
. It holds that

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}(x)}\sumop\displaylimits_{y}|P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{2}(x,y)|-\sumop\displaylimits_{x}|P_{1}(x)-P_{2}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}(x)}\sumop\displaylimits_{y}|P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|.

Proof.

For the second inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)+P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|+|P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x)(P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x))|+|P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)(P_{1}(x)-P_{2}(x))|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}(x)}\sumop\displaylimits_{y}|P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|+\sumop\displaylimits_{x}|P_{1}(x)-P_{2}(x)|.

Meanwhile, for the first inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)+P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}-|P_{1}(x,y)-P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|+|P_{1}(x)P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{x,y}-|P_{1}(x)(P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x))|+|P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)(P_{1}(x)-P_{2}(x))|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}(x)}-\sumop\displaylimits_{y}|P_{1}(y{\,|\,}x)-P_{2}(y{\,|\,}x)|+\sumop\displaylimits_{x}|P_{1}(x)-P_{2}(x)|,

concluding our lemma.
∎

Lemma F.9
.

Consider any two POMDP instances
\mathcal{P}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
and define the belief functions as
\bm{b}_{1:H}
and
\widehat{\bm{b}}_{1:H}
, respectively (see
Appendix

A
for the definition of belief functions). It holds that for any
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{1}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}_{h}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mu_{1}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}\|_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1},

where we remind readers that we denote by
\overline{\tau}_{H}=(s_{1:H},o_{1:H},a_{1:H-1})
the trajectory with states from an episode of the POMDP.

Proof.

The first inequality also appears in
[
20
]
and we provide a simplified proof here for completeness. By
Lemma

F.8
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H-1}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{H-1})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H-1})|+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{H-1},s_{H},o_{H}}\Bigg|\pi_{H-1}(a_{H-1}{\,|\,}\overline{\tau}_{H-1})\mathbb{T}_{H-1}(s_{H}{\,|\,}s_{H-1},a_{H-1})\mathbb{O}_{H}(o_{H}{\,|\,}s_{H})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\pi_{H-1}(a_{H-1}{\,|\,}\overline{\tau}_{H-1})\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{H-1}(s_{H}{\,|\,}s_{H-1},a_{H-1})\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{H}(o_{H}{\,|\,}s_{H})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H-1}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{H-1})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H-1})|+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\left[\|\mathbb{T}_{H-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{H-1},a_{H-1})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{H-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{H-1},a_{H-1})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{H}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{H})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{H}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{H})\|_{1}\right],

where the last step is again from
Lemma

F.8
. Therefore, by repeatedly unrolling the inequality, we proved the first result.

For the second result, we notice that by
Lemma

F.8
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\tau_{h},s_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\tau_{h},s_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\tau_{h},s_{h})|\geq-\sumop\displaylimits_{\tau_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\tau_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\tau_{h})|+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})|.

Notice the fact that
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})
does not depend on
\pi
since it is exactly the belief
\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})(s_{h})
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\bm{b}_{h}(\tau_{h})-\widehat{\bm{b}}_{h}(\tau_{h})\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\tau_{h},s_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\tau_{h},s_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\tau_{h},s_{h})|+\sumop\displaylimits_{\tau_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\tau_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\tau_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|,

(F.9)

where the last step comes from the fact that after marginalization, the total variation distance will not increase. By combining it with the first result, we proved the second result.
∎

Corollary F.10
.

Consider any two POSG instances
\mathcal{G}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
that satisfy
Assumption

2.6
and the corresponding belief functions in the form of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}},\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}:\mathcal{C}_{h}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{P}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}})
for any
h\in[H]
, respectively. It holds that for any
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\|\mu_{1}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}\|_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}.

Proof.

For the second result, we notice that by
Lemma

F.8
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{c_{h},p_{h},s_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h})|\geq-\sumop\displaylimits_{c_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(c_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(c_{h})|+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h},p_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h})|.

Notice the fact that
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h}),\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h})
do not depend on
\pi
due to
Assumption

2.6
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{P}}\|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{c_{h},p_{h}s_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h})|+\sumop\displaylimits_{c_{h}}|\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(c_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|,

(F.10)

where the last step again comes from the fact that after marginalization, the total variation distance will not increase. By combining it with
Lemma

F.9
, we proved the second result.
∎

Lemma F.11
.

For any reward function
r_{1:H}
of
\mathcal{P}
with
r_{h}:{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for any
h\in[H]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}}v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi)\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}v^{\mathcal{P}}(\pi).

Proof.

Denote
\pi^{\star}\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
to be the optimal policy obtained by running value iteration only on the state space
{\mathcal{S}}
for
\mathcal{P}
. Now we are ready to prove the following argument for any
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
inductively:

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{\pi^{\star},\mathcal{P}}(s_{h},a_{h})\geq Q_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h}).

It is easy to see the argument holds for
h=H
. Fix state-action pair
(s_{h},a_{h})\in{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}
and trajectory
(s_{1:h-1},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1})
, we note that:

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{\pi^{\star},\mathcal{P}}(s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[\max_{a_{h+1}}Q_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star},\mathcal{P}}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})\right]

\displaystyle\geq r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
{o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot\mid s_{h+1})}\end{subarray}}\left[\max_{a_{h+1}}Q_{h+1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{1:h+1},o_{1:h+1},a_{1:h+1})\right]

\displaystyle\geq r_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),\\
{o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot\mid s_{h+1})}\end{subarray}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{a_{h+1}\sim\pi_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{1:h+1},o_{1:h+1},a_{1:h})}Q_{h+1}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{1:h+1},o_{1:h+1},a_{1:h+1})\right]

\displaystyle=Q_{h}^{\pi,\mathcal{P}}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h}),

where the first inequality comes from the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Implementation details.

For each problem setting, we generated
20
POMDPs randomly and report the average performance and its standard deviation for each algorithms. For our privileged value learning methods, we find that using a finite memory of
3
already provides strong performance. For our privileged policy learning methods, we instantiate the MDP learning algorithm with the fully observable optimistic natural policy gradient algorithm
[
72
]
. Meanwhile, for both the decoder learning and belief learning, we find that just utilizing all the historic trajectories gives us reasonable performance without additional samples. For baselines, the hyperparameters
\alpha
for
Q
-value update and step size for the policy update are tuned by grid search, where
\alpha
controls the update of temporal difference learning (recall the update rule of temporal difference learning as
Q\leftarrow(1-\alpha)Q+\alpha Q^{\text{target}}
). For asymmetric
Q
-learning, we use
\epsilon
-greedy exploration, where we use the seminal decreasing rate of
\epsilon_{t}=\frac{H+1}{H+t}

[
90
]
. Finally, all simulations are conducted on a personal laptop with Apple M1 CPU and 16 GB memory.

We further experimented on problems of the larger size. In
Figure

3
, we provided additional
9
cases in the following figures corresponds to POMDPs with different combinations of mthe odel size,
S=A=O\in\{4,6,10\}
and
H\in\{10,20,30\}
.
