Appendix H
Missing Details in
Section

7

Proof of
Proposition

7.1
:

Given the condition of
Definition

3.2
, the function
\phi_{h}
that satisfies the condition of
Proposition

7.1
can be constructed recursively as follows for any reachable
\tau_{h}

\displaystyle\phi_{h}(\tau_{h}):=\psi_{h}(\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1}),a_{h-1},o_{h}),

and
\phi_{1}(o_{1}):=\psi_{1}(o_{1})
. Therefore, we can prove by induction that by belief update rule

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})=U_{h}(b^{\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1})};a_{h-1},o_{h})=b^{\psi_{h}(\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1}),a_{h-1},o_{h})},

where the last step is by the definition of our
\phi_{h}
. Therefore, we have
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}=\psi_{h}(\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1}),a_{h-1},o_{h}){\,|\,}\tau_{h})=1
.

For the other direction, it is similar to the proof of Lemma C.1 in
[
27
]
for
m
-step decodable POMDP. Here we prove it for completeness. For any reachable trajectory
\tau_{h}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{h}
, it holds by the belief update and induction that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})=U_{h}(b^{\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1})},a_{h-1},o_{h})=\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h-1}=\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1}),a_{h-1},o_{h}).

Meanwhile, since we know
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(\cdot{\,|\,}\tau_{h})
is a one-hot vector, we can construct
\psi_{h}
such that
\psi_{h}(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h})
is the unique
s_{h}
that makes
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}\tau_{h})>0
with
s_{h-1}=\phi_{h-1}(\tau_{h-1})
. If this procedure does not complete the definition of
\psi
for some
(s_{h-1},a_{h-1},o_{h})
, it implies that either
s_{h-1}
is not reachable or
o_{h}
is not reachable given
s_{h-1}
, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{P}}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h-1},a_{h-1}^{\prime})=0
for any
a_{h-1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
, thus recovering the conditions of
Definition

3.2
.

\hfill\square

Proof of
Proposition

7.3
:

Note that for any given problem instance of a POMDP, we can construct a POSG by adding a dummy agent that has the observation being the exact state at each time step, and has only one dummy action that does not affect the transition or reward. Therefore, even the local private information
p_{i,h}
of the dummy agent can decode the underlying state, and hence
(c_{h},p_{h})
reveals the underlying state.
Therefore, the corresponding POSG with the dummy agent satisfies the condition in
Proposition

7.3
. Meanwhile, it is direct to see that any CCE of the POSG is an optimal policy for the original POMDP. Now by the PSPACE-hardness of planning for POMDPs
[
7
]
, we proved our proposition.

\hfill\square

Theorem H.1
.

Suppose the POSG
\mathcal{G}
satisfies
Definition

7.2
.

For any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
and (potentially stochastic) decoding functions
\widehat{g}=\{\widehat{g}_{i,h}\}_{i\in[n],h\in[H]}
with
\widehat{g}_{i,h}:\mathcal{C}_{h}\times\mathcal{P}_{i,h}\rightarrow\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
for each
i\in[n],h\in[H]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi)\leq 2nH^{2}\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}\max_{j\in[n],h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi)\leq 2nH^{2}\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}\max_{j\in[n],h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

where
\pi^{\widehat{g}}
is the distilled policy of
\pi
through the decoding functions
\widehat{g}
, where at step
h
, each agent
i
firstly individually decodes the state by sampling
s_{h}\sim\widehat{g}_{i,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{i,h})
, and then acts according to the expert
\pi_{i,h}
,
where in the following discussions, we slightly abuse the notation and regard
\widehat{g}_{i,h}(c_{h},p_{i,h})
as a random variable following the distribution of
\widehat{g}_{i,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{i,h})
.
In other words, the decoding process does not need correlations among the agents.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we write
v_{i}
instead of
v_{i}^{\mathcal{G}}
when the underlying model is clear from the context. Firstly, we consider any deterministic decoding function
\widehat{\phi}=\{\widehat{\phi}_{i,h}\}_{i\in[n],h\in[H]}
with
\widehat{\phi}_{i,h}:\mathcal{C}_{h}\times\mathcal{P}_{i,h}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}
for each
i\in[n],h\in[H]
, and note the following that for any
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle v_{i}(\pi)-v_{i}(\pi^{\widehat{\phi}})=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}[R]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}}^{\mathcal{G}}[R]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\forall j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\forall j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]],

where we define
R:=\sumop\displaylimits_{h}r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})
,
and the last step is by the definition of
\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}

\displaystyle v_{i}(\pi)-v_{i}(\pi^{\widehat{\phi}})\leq H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Therefore, by noticing the fact that
\widehat{g}
is equivalent to a mixture of deterministic decoding functions, where at the beginning of each episode, one can firstly independently sample the outcome for each
(c_{h},p_{j,h})
for
j\in[n]
and
h\in[H]
, we conclude that

\displaystyle v_{i}(\pi)-v_{i}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})

\displaystyle=v_{i}(\pi)-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\phi}\sim\widehat{g}}v_{i}(\pi^{\widehat{\phi}})\leq H\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\phi}\sim\widehat{g}}\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle=H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Now we prove our result for NE and CCE first. Due to similar arguments for evaluating
v_{i}(\pi)-v_{i}(\pi^{\widehat{\phi}})
, for any
u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}\cup\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}
, it holds that

\displaystyle v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}})-v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}}}^{\mathcal{G}}[R\mathbbm{1}[\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})]]

\displaystyle\leq H\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

We notice the following fact that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\forall j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))=\sumop\displaylimits_{\bar{\tau}_{H}\in{\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}}_{H}(\widehat{\phi})}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\bar{\tau}_{H}),

where we define
\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{H}(\widehat{\phi}):=\{\overline{\tau}_{H}\in\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{H}{\,|\,}\forall j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})\}
. By definition of
\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\forall\overline{\tau}_{H}\in{\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}}_{H}(\widehat{\phi}):\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})=\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{H}).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\forall j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))=\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\forall j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Correspondingly, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))=\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi^{\widehat{\phi}}_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})),

which implies that

\displaystyle v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}})-v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})

\displaystyle\leq H\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle=H\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Again by the fact that
\widehat{g}
is equivalent to a mixture of deterministic decoding functions, it holds

\displaystyle v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\phi}\sim\widehat{g}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{\phi}})-v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})

\displaystyle\leq H\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\phi}\sim\widehat{g}}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{\phi}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle=H\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

(H.1)

Now we are ready to evaluate the NE/CCE-gap of policy
\pi^{\widehat{g}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})}\right)+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Now we notice that
\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})=\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})
since
\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}\subseteq\Pi_{i}
by the deterministic filter condition
Definition

3.2
and
\pi_{-i}
is a Markov policy. Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{NE/CCE-gap}(\pi)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})}\right)+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}\left({v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-v_{i}(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})}\right)}\right)+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}H\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))}\right)+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq 2H\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}}\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in[n]}\sumop\displaylimits_{h}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq 2nH^{2}\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{i},j\in[n],h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})),

where the second last step is by a union bound, thus proving our result for NE and CCE.

For CE, consider any strategy modification
m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}\cup\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{\widehat{g}})\odot\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h})).

Now we notice that
\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})=\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})
since
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}
by the deterministic filter condition
Definition

7.2
and
Lemma

H.2
. Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi^{\widehat{g}})-\operatorname{CE-gap}(\pi)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{\widehat{g}})\odot\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}\left({v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{\widehat{g}})\odot\pi_{-i}^{\widehat{g}})-v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})}\right)+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}H\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n]\setminus\{i\},h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+H\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\exists j\in[n],h\in[H]:s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2H\max_{i\in[n],m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in[n]}\sumop\displaylimits_{h}\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2nH^{2}\max_{i\in[n],m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i},h\in[H],j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

where the third step is due to the same reason as
Equation

H.1
.
∎

Lemma H.2
.

For any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
, it holds for any
reward function and

i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\text{gen}}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})=\max_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i}).

Proof.

Denote
m_{i}^{\star}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\text{gen}}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})
and
\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})
. Now we shall prove that
V_{i,h}^{(m_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1})\leq V_{i,h}^{(\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h})
inductively for each
h\in[H]
. Note that it holds for
h=H+1
. Now we consider the following

\displaystyle V_{i,h}^{(m_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\\
s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},m_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\Bigg\{r_{h}(s_{h},m_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+V_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{1:h+1},o_{1:h+1},a_{1:h-1},m_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\\
s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},m_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\bigg\{r_{h}(s_{h},m_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{1:h},o_{1:h},a_{1:h-1},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})+V_{i,h+1}^{(\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h+1})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\\
s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},\widehat{m}_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{h},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})\\
o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\end{subarray}}\left[{r_{h}(s_{h},\widehat{m}_{i,h}^{\star}(s_{h},a_{i,h}),a_{-i,h})+V_{i,h+1}^{(\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=V_{i,h}^{(\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}),

where the second inequality follows from the inductive hypothesis and the third step is by the definition of
\widehat{m}_{i}^{\star}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}v_{i}((m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i})
.
∎

Lemma H.3
.

Given an approximate POSG
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
that satisfies
Assumption

2.6
with approximate transitions and emissions being
\{\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h},\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
, we define the approximate decoding function
\widehat{g}
to be

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i,h}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{i,h}):=\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{i,h}),

for each
h\in[H]
,
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
c_{h}\in\mathcal{C}_{h}
,
p_{i,h}\in\mathcal{P}_{i,h}
. Then it holds that for any
\pi\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}}}
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{i},j\in[n],h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right],

and

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i},j\in[n],h\in[H]}\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\max_{i\in[n],m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}\mathbb{E}_{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right].

Proof.

Note for any
i\in[n]
,
u_{i}\in\Pi_{i}
,
j\in[n]
,
h\in[H]
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{j,h})-\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{j,h})\right|,

due to the condition in
Definition

7.2
. Meanwhile,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{j,h})-\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h},p_{j,h})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h},c_{h},p_{j,h}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h},c_{h},p_{j,h})-\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(s_{h},c_{h},p_{j,h})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{h}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(\overline{\tau}_{h})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\widehat{\mathcal{G}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right],

where the first inequality is by the first inequality in
Lemma

H.7
, the second and third inequalities are due to the fact that TV distance does not increase after marginalization, and the last inequality is by
Lemma

F.9
. Since
\pi_{-i}
is a fixed and fully-observable Markov policy, by
Lemma

F.11
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right],

thus proving the first result of our lemma.

For the second result of our lemma, it can be proved similarly that for any
i\in[n]
,
m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}
,
j\in[n]
,
h\in[H]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))\leq\mathbb{E}_{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H-1]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right].

By
Lemma

H.2
, we proved the second result.
∎

Theorem H.4
.

Fix any
\epsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
and
\pi\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}}}
.

Algorithm

5
can learn a decoding function
\widehat{g}
such that with probability
1-\delta

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{i},j\in[n],h\in[H]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))\leq\epsilon,

with total sample complexity
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{nS^{2}AHO+nS^{3}AH}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{S^{4}A^{2}H^{5}}{\epsilon})
and computational complexity
\textsc{poly}(S,A,H,O,\frac{1}{\epsilon}),\log\frac{1}{\delta}
.

Proof.

With the help of
Lemma

H.3
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\right]\leq\epsilon.

The following proof procedure follows similarly to that of
Theorem

F.5
. For each
h\in[H]
and
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
, we define

\displaystyle p_{h}(s_{h})=\max_{i\in[n],u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h}).

Fix
\epsilon_{1},\delta_{1}>0
, we define
\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})=\{s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}{\,|\,}p_{h}(s_{h})\geq\epsilon_{1}\}
. By
[
89
]
, one can learn a policy
\{{}_{i}(h,s_{h})\}_{i\in[n]}
with sample complexity
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{S^{2}A_{i}H^{4}}{\epsilon_{1}})
such that
\max_{i\in[n]}d_{h}^{{}_{i}(h,s_{h})\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})\geq\frac{p_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
for each
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
with probability
1-n\cdot\delta_{1}
. Now we assume this event holds for any
h\in[H]
and
s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})
. For each
s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
, we have executed each policy
\{{}_{i}(h,s_{h})\times\pi_{-i}\}_{i\in[n]}
for the first
h-1
steps followed by an action
a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
for
N
episodes and denote the total number of episodes that
s_{h}
and
a_{h}
are visited as
N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})
, and
N_{h}(s_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}N_{h}(s_{h},a)
. Then, with probability
1-e^{{-}N\epsilon_{1}/8}
, we have
N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\geq\frac{Np_{h}(s_{h})}{2}
by Chernoff bound. Now conditioned on this event, we are ready to evaluate the following for any
i\in[n]
, and
u_{i}\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}},i}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}=\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h},a_{h}}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})(u_{i}\times\pi_{-i})_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1}),a_{h}}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})[u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}]_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1}),a_{h}}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})[u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}]_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}\sqrt{d^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}_{h}(s_{h})}\sqrt{\frac{2S\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+S\sqrt{\frac{2\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}},

where the second step is by
Lemma

H.8
, and the last step is by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}^{\mathcal{G}}\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}=\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{Np_{h}(s_{h})}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h}\in\mathcal{U}(h,\epsilon_{1})}\sqrt{d_{h}^{u_{i}\times\pi_{-i}}(s_{h})}\sqrt{\frac{2O\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot S\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{2SO\log(1/\delta_{2})}{N}},

where the second step is by
Lemma

H.9
, and the last step is by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Therefore, by a union bound, all high probability events hold with probability

\displaystyle 1-SHn\delta_{1}-SHAe^{{-}N\epsilon_{1}/8}-2SAH\delta_{2}.

Therefore, we can choose
N=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{S^{2}+SO}{\epsilon^{2}})
and
\epsilon_{1}=\Theta(\frac{\epsilon}{S})
,
leading to the total sample complexity

\displaystyle SHA\left(nN+\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{S^{3}AH^{4}}{\epsilon}\right)\right)=\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{nS^{2}AHO+nS^{3}AH}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{S^{4}A^{2}H^{5}}{\epsilon}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Note that although our
Algorithm

5
and
Theorem

H.4
are stated for NE/CCE, it can also handle CE with simple modifications, where the key observation is that the strategy modification
m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}},i}
can also be regarded as a Markov policy in an
extended
MDP marginalized by
\pi_{-i}
as defined below.

Definition H.5
.

Fix
\pi\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}}}
.
We define
\mathcal{M}^{\text{extended}}_{i}({\pi})
to be an MDP for agent
i
, where for each
h\in[H]
, the state is
(s_{h},a_{i,h})
, the action is some modified action
a_{i,h}^{\prime}
, the transition is defined as
\mathbb{T}^{\text{extended}}_{h}(s_{h+1},a_{i,h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{i,h},a_{i,h}^{\prime}):=\mathbb{E}_{a_{-i,h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{i,h})}[\mathbb{T}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{i,h}^{\prime},a_{-i,h})\pi_{h+1}(a_{i,h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h+1})]
, where we slightly abuse the notation of
\pi_{h}(a_{-i,h}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{i,h})
and
\pi_{h}(a_{i,h}{\,|\,}s_{h})
by defining them as the posterior and marginal distributions induced by the joint distribution
\pi_{h}(a_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})
. Similarly, the reward is given by
r^{\text{extended}}_{h}(s_{h},a_{i,h},a_{i,h}^{\prime}):=\mathbb{E}_{a_{-i,h}\sim\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{i,h})}[r_{h}(s_{h},a_{i,h}^{\prime},a_{-i,h})]
.

With the help of such an extended MDP, we can develop
Algorithm

6
, which is a CE version of
Algorithm

5
with the following guarantees.

Theorem H.6
.

Fix any
\epsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
and
\pi\in\Pi_{{\mathcal{S}}}
.

Algorithm

6
can learn a decoding function
\widehat{g}
such that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n],m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i},j\in[n],h\in[H]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i})\odot\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(s_{h}\neq\widehat{g}_{j,h}(c_{h},p_{j,h}))\leq\epsilon,

with total sample complexity
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}({\frac{nS^{2}A^{3}HO+nS^{3}A^{4}H}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{S^{4}A^{6}H^{5}}{\epsilon}})
and computational complexity
\textsc{poly}(S,A,H,O,\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

Proof.

Due to the construction of
\mathcal{M}^{\text{extended}}_{i}(\pi)
, the proof of
Theorem

H.4
readily applies, where the only difference is that the state space of
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\text{extended}}(\pi)
is now
SA_{i}
, larger than that of
\mathcal{M}(\pi_{-i})

by a factor of
A_{i}

thus proving our theorem.
∎

We next introduce and prove several supporting lemmas used before.

Lemma H.7
.

Suppose we can sample from a joint distribution
P\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})
for some finite
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
i.i.d. Then we can learn an approximate distribution
Q\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})
with sample complexity
\Theta\left({\frac{|\mathcal{X}||\mathcal{Y}|+\log 1/\delta}{\epsilon^{2}}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim P}\sumop\displaylimits_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(y{\,|\,}x)-Q(y{\,|\,}x)|\leq 2\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-Q(x,y)|\leq\epsilon,

with probability
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Note the following holds

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-Q(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-P(x)Q(y{\,|\,}x)+P(x)Q(y{\,|\,}x)-Q(x,y)|

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-P(x)Q(y{\,|\,}x)|-|P(x)Q(y{\,|\,}x)-Q(x,y)|.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim P}\sumop\displaylimits_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(y{\,|\,}x)-Q(y{\,|\,}x)|

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-Q(x,y)|+\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}Q(y{\,|\,}x)|P(x)-Q(x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-Q(x,y)|.

(H.2)

By the sample complexity of learning discrete distributions
[
83
]
, we can learn
Q
such that
\sumop\displaylimits_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}|P(x,y)-Q(x,y)|\leq\epsilon
in sample complexity
\Theta\left({\frac{|\mathcal{X}||\mathcal{Y}|+\log 1/\delta}{\epsilon^{2}}}\right)
with probability
1-\delta
. Thus, we proved our lemma.
∎

Lemma H.8

(Concentration on transition)
.

Fix
\delta>0
and dataset
\{\overline{\tau}_{H}^{k}\}_{k\in[N]}
sampled from
\mathcal{P}

(or
\mathcal{G}
in the multi-agent setting)
under policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
. We define for each
h\in[H]
,
(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})\in{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}\times{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[N]}\mathbbm{1}[s_{h}^{k}=s_{h},a_{h}^{k}=a_{h}],\qquad N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})=\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[N]}\mathbbm{1}[s_{h}^{k}=s_{h},a_{h}^{k}=a_{h},s_{h+1}^{k}=s_{h}].

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that for any
k\in[K],h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{S\log(SAHK/\delta)}{\max\{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1\}}},

for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
, where we define
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})=\frac{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h},s_{h+1})}{\max\{N_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),1\}}
.

Proof.

This is done by firstly bounding
\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}
for specific
k,h,s_{h},a_{h}
according to
[
83
]
, and then taking union bound for all
k\in[K],h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Lemma H.9

(Concentration on emission)
.

Fix
\delta>0
and dataset
\{\overline{\tau}_{H}^{k}\}_{k\in[N]}
sampled from
\mathcal{P}

(or
\mathcal{G}
in the multi-agent setting)
under some policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
. We define for each
h\in[H]
,
(s_{h},o_{h})\in{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle N_{h}(s_{h},o_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[N]}\mathbbm{1}[s_{h}^{k}=s_{h},o_{h}^{k}=o_{h}],\qquad N_{h}(s_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[N]}\mathbbm{1}[s_{h}^{k}=s_{h}].

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{O\log(SHK/\delta)}{\max\{N_{h}^{k}(s_{h}),1\}}},

for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
,
where we define
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(o_{h}{\,|\,}s_{h})=\frac{N_{h}(s_{h},o_{h})}{\max\{N_{h}(s_{h}),1\}}
.

Proof.

This is done by firstly bounding
\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}
for specific
k,h,s_{h}
according to
[
83
]
, and then taking union bound for all
k\in[K],h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
.
∎

Now we switch to proving the guarantees for
Algorithm

7
.

Lemma H.10
.

Fix
\delta>0
. With probability
1-\delta
, it holds that for any
k\in[K],h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}}
:

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{o_{h+1}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k}_{h}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\right|\leq C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{O\log(SHKA/\delta)}{N_{h}^{k}(s_{h},a_{h})}},

where
\widehat{\mathbb{J}}_{h}^{k}
is defined in
Algorithm

7
.

Proof.

This is done by firstly bounding
\sumop\displaylimits_{o_{h+1}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k}_{h}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\right|
for specific
k,h,s_{h},a_{h}
according to
[
83
]
, and then taking union bound for all
k\in[K],h\in[H],s_{h}\in{\mathcal{S}},a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
.
∎

From now on, we shall use the bonus

\displaystyle b_{h}^{k}(s_{h},a_{h})=\min\left\{C_{3}(H-h)\sqrt{\frac{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}{\max\{N_{h}^{k}(s_{h},a_{h}),1\}}},2(H-h)\right\}

(H.3)

in
Algorithm

7
,
for some absolute constant
C_{3}>0
.

Before presenting our technical analysis, we define the following notation for the ease of presentation. We define the following approximate value functions for any policy
\pi\in\Pi
in a backward way for
h\in[H]

when given some approximate belief in the form of
\{\widehat{P}_{h}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{P}_{h}\times{\mathcal{S}})\}_{h\in[H]}
as discussed in
Section

7.2
:

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}):=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h},\{a_{j,h}\sim\pi_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},c_{h},p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+V_{i,h+1}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}):=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+V_{i,h+1}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],

for each
(i,c_{h})\in[n]\times\mathcal{C}_{h}
and
\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
, where we define
\widehat{V}_{i,H+1}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{H+1})=0
.

Intuitively, this definition of
\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
mimics the Bellman equation of the ground-truth value function
V_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
by replacing the ground-truth belief
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}c_{h})
by
\widehat{P}_{h}(s_{h},p_{h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})
. Next, we point out the following quantitative bound when using
\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
to approximate
V_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
.

Lemma H.11
.

For any
\pi^{\prime},\pi\in\Pi
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{G}}\big|V_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})-\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})\big|\leq(H-h+1)^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}},

where

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\text{belief}}:=\max_{h\in[H]}\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\left\|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})-\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})\right\|_{1}.

Proof.

It follows directly by combining Lemma 4 and Lemma 8 of
[
29
]
.
∎

Meanwhile, note that although in
Algorithm

7
, the value functions we maintain have input
\widehat{c}_{h}
instead of
c_{h}
for computational efficiency, we extend the definitions of those value functions to also accept
c_{h}
as inputs as follows (with a slight abuse of notation):

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle:=Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}),\quad Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h}):=Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h}),\quad V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}):=V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(\widehat{c}_{h})

\displaystyle Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle:=Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}),\quad Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h}):=Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h}),\quad V_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}):=V_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(\widehat{c}_{h}),

where we recall that
\widehat{c}_{h}=\text{Compress}_{h}(c_{h})
.

Lemma H.12

(Optimism 1 for NE/CCE)
.

With probability
1-\delta
, for any
k\in[K]
, for
Algorithm

7
, it holds that for any
i\in[n]
,
\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}
,
h\in[H]

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{Q}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}),\qquad V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h})\geq\widehat{V}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h}),

where we recall that
\widehat{c}_{h}=\text{Compress}_{h}(c_{h})
.

Proof.

We will prove by backward induction. Obviously, it holds for
h=H+1
. Now we assume the lemma holds for
h+1
, then by definition

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\left[{Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\min\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\min\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\},

where the last step is by inductive hypothesis. Now note that for any
(s_{h},p_{h},a_{h})
, we have

\displaystyle b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\geq b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-(H-h)\|\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],

where we notice
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h+1}}\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\mathbb{T}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})
for the first inequality, and the second inequality comes from the construction of our bonus
b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})
in
Equation

H.3
and
Lemma

H.10
. Meanwhile, by the definition of value functions, it holds that
\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]\leq H-h
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\min

\displaystyle\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\}

\displaystyle\geq r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right].

Now we conclude

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}[r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})]

\displaystyle=\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}).

By definition, we have
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})=Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})
, thus proving
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})\geq\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})
.
Now for the value function, note that

\displaystyle V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h},\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h},\pi_{i,h}^{\prime}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{i,h}^{\prime},c_{h},\cdot),\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]\setminus\{i\}})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\pi_{i,h}^{\prime}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{i,h}^{\prime},c_{h},\cdot),\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]\setminus\{i\}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}),

where the first step is by the property of Bayesian CCE, and the second step is by
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})\geq\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})
for any
\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
as proved above. Again by definition, we proved
V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(\widehat{c}_{h})=V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h})\geq\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
.
∎

Lemma H.13

(Optimism 1 for CE)
.

With probability
1-\delta
, for any
k\in[K]
, for
Algorithm

7
, it holds that for any
i\in[n]
,
m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}
,
h\in[H]

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{Q}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}),\qquad V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h})\geq\widehat{V}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h}).

Proof.

We will prove by backward induction. Obviously, it holds for
h=H+1
. Now we assume the lemma holds for
h+1
.
Now we notice that by definition,

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\left[{Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\min\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\min\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\},

where the last step is by inductive hypothesis. Now note that for any
s_{h},p_{h},a_{h}
, we have

\displaystyle b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\geq b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-(H-h)\|\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],

where we notice
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{s_{h+1}}\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(o_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h+1})\mathbb{T}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})
for the first inequality, and the second inequality comes from the construction of our bonus
b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})
in
Equation

H.3
and
Lemma

H.10
. Meanwhile, by the definition of value functions, it holds that
\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]\leq H-h
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\min\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1},o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],H-h+1\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\geq r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right].

Now we conclude

\displaystyle Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle=\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}).

By definition, we have
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})=Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})
, thus proving
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})\geq\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})
.
Now for the value function, note that

\displaystyle V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h},\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h},(m_{i,h}\diamond\pi_{i,h}^{k})(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{i,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot),\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]\setminus\{i\}})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},(m_{i,h}\diamond\pi_{i,h}^{k})(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{i,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot),\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]\setminus\{i\}})

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}),

where the first step is by the property of Bayesian CE, and the second step is by
Q^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})\geq\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})
for any
\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
as proved above. Again by definition, we proved
V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(\widehat{c}_{h})=V_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h})\geq\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{(m_{i}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h})
.
∎

Lemma H.14

(Pessimism)
.

With probability
1-\delta
, for any
k\in[K]
, for
Algorithm

7
, it holds that for any
i\in[n]
,
h\in[H]

\displaystyle Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{Q}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}),\qquad V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(\widehat{c}_{h})\leq\widehat{V}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h}).

Proof.

We prove by backward induction on
h
. Obviously, the lemma holds for
h=H+1
. Now we assume the lemma holds for
h+1
. Similar to the proof of the previous lemma, we note by inductive hypothesis

\displaystyle Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\max\left\{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})-b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1},o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],0\right\},

where for any
s_{h}
,
p_{h}
,
a_{h}
, we have

\displaystyle-b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq-b^{k-1}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})+(H-h)\|\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h}),o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{O}_{h+1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h+1})}\left[{\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right],

where the last step again comes from the construction of our bonus in
Equation

H.3
and
Lemma

H.10
. Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\gamma_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}^{k-1}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})}\left[{r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})+\widehat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h+1})}\right]=\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\gamma_{h}).

Similarly, for value function, it holds that

\displaystyle V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}Q^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h},\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})\leq\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\widehat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h},\{\pi^{k}_{j,h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})=\widehat{V}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}_{i,h}(c_{h}),

thus proving our lemma.
∎

Theorem H.15

(NE/CCE version)
.

With probability
1-\delta
,
Algorithm

7
enjoys the regret guarantee of

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}}V_{i,1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})}\right)\leq\mathcal{O}(KH^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+H^{2}\sqrt{SAOK\log(SAHK/\delta)}+H^{2}SA\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}).

Correspondingly, this implies that one can learn an
(\epsilon+H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}})
-NE if
\mathcal{G}
is zero-sum and
(\epsilon+H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}})
-CCE if
\mathcal{G}
is general-sum with sample complexity
\mathcal{O}(\frac{H^{4}SAO\log(SAHO/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}})
and computation complexity
\textsc{poly}(S,\max_{h\in[H]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[H]}|\mathcal{P}_{h}|,H,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log\frac{1}{\delta})
.

Proof.

Note for any given
i\in[n]
and
\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}
, by
Lemma

H.12
and
Lemma

H.14
, it holds

\displaystyle\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}}V_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}^{k})-V_{i,h}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}^{k})\leq V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k}).

Therefore, it suffices to bound
V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})
:

\displaystyle V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h}^{k})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\left[{Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},\{\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},\{\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h}^{k})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\left[{Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},\{\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},\{\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})}\right]+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h}^{k})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\left[{Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})}\right]+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq Z_{k,h}^{1}+Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq Z_{k,h}^{1}+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\widehat{\mathbb{J}}_{h}^{k-1}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1})-V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1})}\right]+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})+2b_{h}^{k-1}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq Z_{k,h}^{1}+\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1})-V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1})}\right]+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})+3b_{h}^{k-1}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq Z_{k,h}^{1}+Z_{k,h}^{2}+V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1}^{k})-V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1}^{k})+(H-h+1)\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})+3b_{h}^{k-1}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}),

where we define the two Martingale difference sequences as follows

\displaystyle Z_{k,h}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{s_{h},p_{h}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h}^{k})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega_{h}}\mathbb{E}_{\{a_{j,h}\sim\pi_{j,h}^{k}(\cdot{\,|\,}\omega_{j,h},\widehat{c}_{h}^{k},p_{j,h})\}_{j\in[n]}}\bigg[Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h},s_{h},a_{h})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\left({Q_{i,h}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-Q_{i,h}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h}^{k},p_{h}^{k},s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\right)

\displaystyle Z_{k,h}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{o_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[{V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1})-V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1})}\right]-\left({V_{i,h+1}^{\text{high},k}(c_{h+1}^{k})-V_{i,h+1}^{\text{low},k}(c_{h+1}^{k})}\right),

and the error of the belief is defined as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k}):=\|\widehat{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h}^{k})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h}^{k})\|_{1}.

Since
|Z_{k,h}^{1}|\leq H
,
|Z_{k,h}^{2}|\leq H
, and
\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})\leq 2
, by Azuma-Hoeffding bound, we conclude with probability
1-3\delta
, the following holds

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k,h}Z_{k,h}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(H\sqrt{HK\log\frac{1}{\delta}}),\qquad\qquad\sumop\displaylimits_{k,h}Z_{k,h}^{2}\leq\mathcal{O}(H\sqrt{HK\log\frac{1}{\delta}}),

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k,h}\epsilon_{h}(c_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{k}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{k}}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[{\sumop\displaylimits_{h}\epsilon_{h}(c_{h})}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{HK\log\frac{1}{\delta}})\leq KH\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{HK\log\frac{1}{\delta}}).

Meanwhile, by the pigeonhole principle, it holds that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k,h}b_{h}^{k-1}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\leq H\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}\sumop\displaylimits_{k,h}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\max\{1,N_{h}^{k-1}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathcal{O}\left({H\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}(H\sqrt{SAK}+HSA)}\right).

Now by
Lemma

H.12
and
Lemma

H.14
and putting everything together, we conclude

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}}\widehat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})-\widehat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})}\right)\leq KH^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+\mathcal{O}(H^{2}\sqrt{SAOK\log(SAHK/\delta)}+H^{2}SA\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}).

Now by
Lemma

H.11
, we proved the regret guarantees as follows

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}}V_{i,1}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\mathcal{O}(KH^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+H^{2}\sqrt{SAOK\log(SAHK/\delta)}+H^{2}SA\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}).

For the PAC guarantees, since we define
k^{\star}\in\arg\min_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}V^{\text{high},k}_{i,1}(c_{1}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,1}(c_{1}^{k})
, we have

\displaystyle\text{CCE-gap}(\pi^{k^{\star}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}})+\max_{i\in[n]}\left(V^{\text{high},k^{\star}}_{i,h}(c_{1}^{k^{\star}})-V^{\text{low},k^{\star}}_{i,h}(c_{1}^{k^{\star}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}})+\left(\frac{1}{K}\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[K]}V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{1}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{1}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+H^{2}\sqrt{SAO\log(SAHK/\delta)/K}+\frac{H^{2}SA}{K}\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}).

Finally, for two-player zero-sum games, we denote
\widehat{\pi}^{k^{\star}}
to be the marginalized policy of
\pi^{k^{\star}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\text{NE-gap}(\widehat{\pi}^{k^{\star}})\leq\text{CCE-gap}(\pi^{k^{\star}}),

thus concluding our theorem.
∎

Theorem H.16

(CE version)
.

With probability
1-\delta
,
Algorithm

7
enjoys the regret guarantee of

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in[n]}\left({\max_{m_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}}V_{i,1}^{(m_{i}^{\prime}\diamond\pi_{i}^{k})\odot\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{1}^{k})}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\leq\mathcal{O}(KH^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}}+H^{2}\sqrt{SAOK\log(SAHK/\delta)}+H^{2}SA\sqrt{O\log(SAHK/\delta)}).

Correspondingly, this implies that one can learn an
(\epsilon+H^{2}\epsilon_{\text{belief}})
-CE with sample complexity
\mathcal{O}(\frac{H^{4}SAO\log(SAHO/\delta)}{\epsilon^{2}})

and computation complexity
\textsc{poly}(S,\max_{h\in[H]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[H]}|\mathcal{P}_{h}|,H,\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log\frac{1}{\delta})

Proof.

Then proof follows as that of
Theorem

H.15
, where we only need to change the first step of the proof as

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\times\pi_{-i}^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}^{k})-\widehat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi^{k},\mathcal{G}}(c_{h}^{k})\leq V^{\text{high},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k})-V^{\text{low},k}_{i,h}(c_{h}^{k}),

by
Lemma

H.13
and
Lemma

H.14
, and the remaining steps are exactly the same.
∎

Lemma H.17

(Adapted from
Theorem

F.5
)
.

Algorithm
8
can learn the approximate POMDP with transition
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{1:H}
and emission
\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{1:H}
such that for any policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\text{gen}}
and
h\in[H]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}\left[{\|\mathbb{T}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})\|_{1}+\|\mathbb{O}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\widehat{\mathbb{O}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\|_{1}}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}(\epsilon),

using sample complexity
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{S^{2}AHO+S^{3}AH}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{S^{4}A^{2}H^{5}}{\epsilon})
with probability
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Note that
Algorithm

8
is essentially treating the POSG
\mathcal{G}
as a centralized MDP and running
Algorithm

4
, where the only modifications we make in
Algorithm

8
is that we take the controller set
(see some examples of the controller set in
Section

A.3
)
into considerations when learning the models. Specifically, for the transition
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}
, what we estimate is only
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{\mathcal{I}_{h},h})
instead of
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{h}(s_{h+1}{\,|\,}s_{h},a_{h})
, where
{\mathcal{T}}_{h}\subseteq[n]
is the controller set. Therefore, the sample complexity of
Algorithm

8
will not be worse than that of
Algorithm

4
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

7.7
:

Note that the proof idea essentially resembles that of
Theorem

F.6
, where we construct the model
\mathcal{G}^{\text{trunc}}
for
\mathcal{G}
in exactly the same way as constructing
\mathcal{P}^{\text{trunc}}
for
\mathcal{P}
. Therefore,
by
Corollary

F.10
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}^{\mathcal{G}}[\|\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{G}^{\text{trunc}}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})\|_{1}]\leq 2\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{\tau}_{H}}|\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})-\mathbb{P}^{\pi,\mathcal{G}^{\text{trunc}}}(\overline{\tau}_{H})|\leq 4HS\epsilon_{1}.

Meanwhile, we can construct
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{\text{trunc}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{\text{sub}}
using exactly the same way as for
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{trunc}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{\text{sub}}
, where
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{\text{sub}}
is a
\gamma/2
-observable POSG.

Now, according to
[
29
]
, for all the examples in
Section

A.3
, there exists a compression function that maps
c_{h}
to
\widehat{c}_{h}
such that the size of the compressed common information is quasi-polynomial, i.e.,
\widehat{C}_{h}\leq(AO)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log\frac{SH}{\epsilon_{2}}}
for some absolute constant
C
and
\epsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
, and the corresponding approximate belief
\{\widehat{P}_{h}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\rightarrow\Delta({\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{P}_{h})\}_{h\in[H]}
satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{\text{sub}}}_{\pi}\|\mathbb{P}^{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}^{\text{sub}}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}c_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})\|_{1}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, we can do the same augmentation for
\widetilde{P}_{h}
on states from
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{\text{low}}
to construct the approximate belief
\widehat{P}_{h}
as in the proof of
Theorem

F.6
, and the remaining steps follow from the proof of
Theorem

F.6
. This will lead to a total of polynomial-time and polynomial-sample complexities.

\hfill\square

H.1
Background on Bayesian Games

The Bayesian game is a generalization of normal-form games in partially observable settings. Specifically, a Bayesian game is specified as
(n,\{\mathcal{A}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]},\{\Theta_{i}\}_{i\in[n]},\{r_{i}\}_{i\in[n]},\mu)
, where
n
is the number of players,
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is the actor space,
\Theta_{i}
is the type space,
r_{i}:\times_{i\in[n]}(\Theta_{i}\times\mathcal{A}_{i})\rightarrow[0,1]
is the reward function, and
\mu
is the prior distribution of the joint type. At the beginning of the game, a type
\theta=(\theta_{i})_{i\in[n]}
is drawn from the prior distribution
\mu\in\Delta(\Theta)
. Then each agent
i
gets its own type
\theta_{i}
and takes the action
a_{i}
. We define a pure strategy of an agent as
s_{i}\in\text{ST}_{i}:=\{\Theta_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{i}\}
. We define
J_{i}(s_{i},s_{-i})
to be the expected rewards for agent
i
, given the pure joint strategy
(s_{i},s_{-i})
.

By definition,
J_{i}(s_{i},s_{-i})
can be evaluated as

\displaystyle J_{i}(s_{i},s_{-i}):=\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim\mu}r_{i}(\theta,s_{i}(\theta_{i}),s_{-i}(\theta_{-i})).

Bayesian NE.

We define
\gamma^{\star}\in\times_{i\in[n]}\Delta(\text{ST}_{i})
is an
\epsilon
-NE is it satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(s)\geq\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(s_{i}^{\prime},s_{-i})-\epsilon,~~~~\forall i\in[n],s_{i}^{\prime}\in\text{ST}_{i}.

Bayesian CCE.

We say a distribution of joint strategies
\gamma^{\star}\in\Delta(\times_{i\in[n]}\text{ST}_{i})
to be a
\epsilon
-Bayesian CCE if it satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(s)\geq\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(s_{i}^{\prime},s_{-i})-\epsilon,~~~~\forall i\in[n],s_{i}^{\prime}\in\text{ST}_{i}.

(Agent-form) Bayesian CE.

We say a distribution of joint strategies
\gamma^{\star}\in\Delta(\times_{i\in[n]}\text{ST}_{i})
to be an
\epsilon
-agent-form Bayesian CE if it satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(s)\geq\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\gamma^{\star}}J_{i}(m_{i}\diamond s_{i},s_{-i})-\epsilon,~~~~\forall i\in[n],m_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{i},

where
\mathcal{M}_{i}=\{\Theta_{i}\times\mathcal{A}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{i}\}
is the space for strategy modification, where
m_{i}
modifies
s_{i}
as follows: given current type
\theta_{i}
and the recommended action
a_{i}
, the strategy modification changes the action to the another action
m_{i}(\theta_{i},a_{i})
.

Note that Bayesian NE for zero-sum games, and (agent-form) Bayesian CE/CCE are all tractable solution concepts and can be computed with polynomial computational complexity, e.g.,
[
91
,
92
,
93
]
.
